# Read First: The Connected World Of 2026-10-09

**This section and the scope reset's below it supersede everything after them.** The owner's brief, the same day
as the reset: "Connect And Perfect The Current World — Without Restoring Removed Systems". Nothing removed in the
reset came back. What the reset kept was connected where a student's life has the thing, and what only a removed
system could reach was retired with its reason, never quietly.

## Where it is

- **Branch:** `claude/connect-world`, fast-forwarded into `main`. Commits: `2edc8b60` (part one) and the ones after it.
- **Baseline kept:** `backup/pre-scope-reset-2026-10-09` and the tag of the same name, at `81f77592`.
- **Save schema 91**, both pins moved (`model.ts`, `base44/shared/guard.js`). `migrate` sheds on every open
  (`GONE_AT_91`): the rent's and bills' ways of paying and checks, the union plans and COBRA, reputation and
  recognition, the return home, a wheelchair and a parking permit, a card's stale billers, and a home's, a lodger's
  and a farm's lines on a year's earnings. A rent paid ahead is still given back. Tested in `tests/simulation.test.ts`.
- **Not deployed.** A deployment of an older line refuses schema 91 saves (see §10 further down for Base44).

## The deliverables, and where each is

- **Coverage matrix:** `docs/capabilities.json` (and its printout `docs/CAPABILITIES.md`, kept by
  `tools/capabilities.mjs`): 34 rows for the current world naming all 111 commands, each with its goal, entry,
  state, preconditions, effects, what persists, its readers, its drill-down looks and routes, its save/load test,
  provenance, tests, status and limits. 27 Verified, 7 Partial (each says why it can't be reached in a student's
  life), 1 Retired (Getting About). The rows of the brief of 2026-10-08 stay Retired as the record.
- **Features and their readers:** the matrix's `readers` column; `docs/TRACEABILITY.md` and `docs/DEVMAP.md` for
  every command's screen, test and journey.
- **Creation audit:** every field has a role it says. Dispositions: Extraversion reads a house watching
  (`takeTheHouse`, at every performance's nerves), Openness new work (a festival's new play, a Cabaret piece),
  Agreeableness a rehearsal, the other four the knobs. Interests and genres read only where a student's life has
  the thing (`interestsRead`): the plays are filed by kind (`genresOfPlay`), coursework by its craft
  (`Template.crafts`), a play read by Reading; the rest are marked · at creation, said back only. Race and
  ethnicity, a middle name, a background and a portrait decide nothing, and the screen says so (the portrait's
  hint now says it's never looked at). Weight is disclosed for what it does: chronic onset and the mortality
  hazard. Pronouns set the part a play writes for them (measured below).
- **Drill-downs:** a trait (what each knob moves, its own figure and with all you are), a condition carried (what
  it does left alone, the dial it takes from), a piece of coursework (each part's preparation against its weight,
  due day, course), beside the existing readiness, dials, calendar entries, accounts, transactions, days and
  holidays, weather. The person look lists traits and conditions; a course lists its open work.
- **Look After Yourself:** `lookAfterOptions` in `systems/life.ts`. Order: what's costing the body every hour
  (`HUNGRY`, `GRIMY`, `OVERWROUGHT`) first, marked Needed Now; then by readiness given back; then little to gain;
  then refused, with the command's own words. Ties keep `LOOKING_AFTER`'s order. Each shows its time, cost and
  the dials it would move, the preview being `applyLookingAfter` on a copy. Nothing happens unchosen; asking moves
  no clock, money or dial (`tests/look-after.test.ts`).
- **Leftovers resolved:** `docs/UNBITTEN.md`, "The scope reset of 2026-10-09", every item with what was done.
- **Journeys:** `tests/journey-money.test.ts` (a year's money at Wells Fargo through the bank's commands, saved
  midway, the return filed) and `tests/journey-life.test.ts` (creation saved and opened at once, plans, a play read,
  the menu, care, papers, through the 2028 leap day). Plus the three school journeys.
- **Long lives:** `tools/lives.ts` (`npx vite-node tools/lives.ts -- --seeds 3 --years 3`, and `--pairs`).

- **Defects fixed on the way:** `tsc --noEmit` at the root checked nothing (the root config only holds references;
  `tsc -b` is the check); New Haven had no therapist; a new card's notice warned of declined bills that can't happen;
  the guide promised a wheelchair's parking permit and stale bills; a calendar entry was named with its year
  (The Fall Term Begins, now); Desempleo's panel offered a claim nothing can file; and the long-life probe's own
  policies were wrong (an hour-step autopilot never sleeps, by design; a sleep only at eleven missed late calls).

## Verification (2026-10-09)

- `npm run gate`, read from a file: exit 0. 110 test files, 1,375 tests, typecheck (`tsc -b`), lint, all fifteen checks.
- Inventory: 111 commands, 64 scheduled kinds. Traceability: 46 commands verified in a journey, 65 tested, 0 partial.
- Mutations: 14 of this phase's claims mutated, each killed by its test. One survived at first, the menu's urgency,
  and the test that tells urgency from gain was added.
- `vite build`: exit 0. The built app walked through `tools/screenshot.mjs` at 1440 and 390 wide: creation (the
  interests marked ·), Home with the ordered Look After Yourself menu open, School, Coursework, Papers. No sideways
  scroll on the phone.
- Long lives (`tools/lives.ts`, three years each, saved and opened every new year): 15 lives over five profiles (New
  York, Mexico City, London with Early Riser and Curious, Reykjavík on autopilot, and a test-setup student without
  family money): 0 breaches, 0 unbalanced ledgers, 12 graduated, 3 dismissed.
- Pairs (`--pairs`, eight seeds as an actor and as an actress, otherwise alike): parts cast, actor 18 leads, 11
  supporting, 7 featured; actress 20, 6 and 8. No penalty by pronouns.

## The open items, finished (the owner: "Finish and complete those things in open perfectly")

1. **The dismissals were the probe's, not the game's.** `tools/lives.ts`'s player prepared only at eight in the
   evening, often while at a call, and never learned its parts, so its festival nights went off and its growth
   lagged. Given a student's free hours (the part first: a note to work, blocking missed, lines; then the work due
   soonest), the same life (`pair-1`, as an actor) graduated in good standing every term, ahead of the class most
   terms, readiness 94 at nine. Rerun, the actor/actress pairs (eight seeds each, three years): **16 of 16
   graduated**, 0 breaches, 0 unbalanced ledgers; parts cast, actor 22 leads, 16 supporting, 2 featured; actress 28,
   9, 3. Neither is held back; the sample is small. `school.learn_part` now reads the hour (`focusAt`), as every
   other step does. The profile rerun, one seed a profile for 3.3 years so the months after the degree are in it,
   the graduate enrolling in cover when Yale Health ends (`keepCovered`): all five graduated, 0 breaches, 0
   unbalanced ledgers. The two US citizens went on Medicaid; the Mexican, British and Icelandic graduates fell out of
   status at the grace period's end and were refused cover, as the marketplace's lawful-presence rule has it. Each
   wealthy life ended with about $75,000 in the bank: savings untouched for three years went to Connecticut as
   unclaimed property (`accountlife.ts`, three years, as the state's law has it), told as dormant first and claimable
   back (`bank.claim_unclaimed`). The three-year runs before it stopped just short of that.
2. **Papers connected to the kept world:** a new bank asks for a photo ID in force (`photoIdOf`, 31 CFR 1020.220);
   the marketplace, the Essential Plan and Covered Connecticut ask lawful presence (`lawfullyPresent`, 45 CFR
   155.305), so OPT and falling out of status now change a graduate's cover; New Haven takes a police report online
   (Police to Citizen, read secondhand); a lost license is replaced at its issuer's fee, free in New York stolen with
   the police's report (MV-78B, `documents.replace_license`).
3. **Retired with reasons:** applying for visas and travel authorizations, booking the visit an application waited
   on, and making a license REAL ID: each was for crossing a border or boarding a plane, and nothing does since the
   reset. What a country asks stays on Papers as information; a pending one in an older save is still decided.
4. **Measured and true to the rules, so left as they are:** a student's work-study wages are kept out of
   unemployment's base period (FUTA §3306(c)(10)(B)); no American bank arranges an overdraft, and a loan is made
   against wages paid in; a US-born student holds a passport or a license at the published shares.
5. **Still out of reach, each in `docs/capabilities.json`'s limits:** the foreign banks' rights, the pensions,
   most benefits, the residence routes beyond Mexico's, and OPT's end (90 days without work, since nothing after the
   degree employs anybody). Each needs income after the degree or a life outside the school, which the owner's brief
   held back (the career, travel and moving are not to come back). That's the owner's call to make.

## Verification of the finishing work (2026-10-09)

- `npm run gate`, read from a file: exit 0. 110 test files, 1,376 tests, typecheck, lint, all fifteen checks.
- Inventory: 109 commands. Traceability: 46 verified in a journey, 63 tested, 0 partial. Capabilities: 27 Verified,
  6 Partial, 19 Retired.
- Mutations: the six new claims (photo ID, lawful presence in enrollment, in the programs and in a plan held, the
  MV-78B waiver, New Haven's report), each killed by its test; with the earlier fourteen, 20 of 20.

# Read First: The Scope Reset Of 2026-10-09

**This section supersedes everything below it.** Everything after it was written for the line that ran to
`81f77592`. Any instruction in it, or in an older handoff, to build, restore or expand the systems listed as removed
here is superseded by the owner's reset. Its open-work lists are history, not a backlog.

## What the owner asked, in order (2026-10-09)

1. The reset brief: keep creation, banking and school; remove the personal life and the profession.
2. A correction the same day: keep daily living too (sleep, washing, food, weather), and a simple residence.
3. The final word, which governs: "get back to this setup/engine in that full page capture", the game as it stood
   at the end of 2026-09-29 (`7e976601`). Keep "the current money/banking engine, school engine, and papers engine",
   and remove the home, travel, cars and land. Then: "You can remove the rented room and household too."

## What the game is now

- **Base:** the setup of 2026-09-29 (`7e976601`), meaning its Home page, daily life (sleep, eating, ordering in, a
  shower, a workout, unwinding, the body, illness and injury), creation, guide, calendar, weather, birthdays and
  mortality.
- **Today's engines** (`81f77592`):
  - **Banking:** banking, banknumber, accountlife, statements, cash, credit, lending, cardlife, cardoffers,
    payments, pending, security and finance.
  - **School:** school, coursework, learning, production, classmates and playreading.
  - **Papers:** health, appointments, clinics, therapy, access, taxreturn, earnings, unemployment, the benefits,
    the state pensions, documents, identity, permission, residence, citizenship and papervisits.
- **Removed:**
  - the home and everything about owning, renting or moving;
  - the rented room and the household's rent and bills;
  - furniture and appliances;
  - travel: journeys, flights, rail, stays and jet lag;
  - cars: vehicles, driving and licensing;
  - land, construction and permits;
  - **the career**, meaning its agencies, commercials, classmates' careers and the SAG pension. The owner's
    correction had removed the professional career, its location work and relocation stood on travel and the
    home, and the final word didn't bring it back.
  - everything the line of 2026-10-09 had built after 2026-09-29 outside the three kept engines: the shops and
    catalogs, belongings, clothes, the kitchen, routines, the people and family, the industry and its database,
    and the rail.
- **Money with no home:** `systems/spending.ts` holds what every payment goes through (bank, card, cash, owed)
  and the day's food, bought each morning, with SNAP on an EBT card first. "Where they live" is where they are,
  since there's no travel to tell them apart.
- **Graduation** ends at the school's results (`school.endedAt`). The Yale Health plan runs to the July after it.
- **Every life** arms its tax returns and its health. The career used to do that for a life begun outside school.
- **Moved screens:** the state pension claims are on Papers (Unemployment), and the real plays to read are on
  School (Coursework).
- **Home** is the September 29 layout without its Home, Career, Travel, Cars and Land cards. The rail is Home
  only, as it was then, with Find A Place.

## Saves

- **Schema 90** (91 since the connected world, above), moved in both places: `SAVE_SCHEMA_VERSION` in `src/engine/domain/model.ts` and
  `SUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSION` in `base44/shared/guard.js`.
- **What `migrate` does:**
  - Sheds 107 fields of removed systems from every person (`GONE_AT_90` in `engine/index.ts`) and a classmate's
    career.
  - Lets go every pending calendar event no handler takes and every open decision whose action no command takes.
  - Moves a home's fed morning onto the person.
  - Refuses, unchanged, a save whose ledger doesn't reconcile, with a message saying so.
  - On first opening, returns a home's deposit and any rent paid ahead to the current account. What was owed on
    rent stays owed, in the same account, and is paid down from the bank.
- **Kept:** identity, money and every ledger posting, the school, the papers and the body.
- A save from a newer build is refused as before.

## Where things are

- **Baseline:** branch `backup/pre-scope-reset-2026-10-09` and tag `pre-scope-reset-2026-10-09`, both at
  `81f77592`, on GitHub.
- **The first reset attempt** (the correction-scope keep list), abandoned when the owner chose the 2026-09-29 base:
  `bb4d1700` on `claude/scope-reset`. It doesn't typecheck and is kept only to be read.
- **This work:** `claude/sep29-reset`. The code is `bcc30c88`; the records and gate fixes come in the commit
  after it.

## Verification

- `npm run gate`, read from a file, exit 0, run twice on the final tree with `VITEST_MAX_FORKS=2`: 105 test files and 1,346 tests, typecheck, lint, and all fifteen checks. catalog-coverage left the gate with the catalogs.
- Inventory: 117 commands, 65 scheduled kinds and 4 routes. Traceability: 9 commands verified in a journey, 88 implemented but unverified and 20 partial.
- Production build (`vite build`): exit 0.
- In the built app, through `tools/screenshot.mjs` on a new life with the guide read, at 1440 and 390 wide: creation; Home, matching the 2026-09-29 page less its Home, Career, Travel, Cars and Land cards; School; Bank; Papers; the Calendar. There is no sideways scroll on the phone.
- Tests for removed systems were deleted; cases about rent, licences, travel and the career were cut from kept files. The heat index was ported back and mutation-tested.
- Not run: the long replay and the multi-seed lives (see the limits below).

## Known gaps and limits

- `docs/UNBITTEN.md`, "The scope reset of 2026-10-09", lists what the reset leaves declared and biting nothing:
  - presence, reputation and recognition, which the school still writes and only the career read (the School screen shows composure alone);
  - checks and a direct debit taken back, with no rent or bills to come from;
  - the rent and bills payment settings;
  - card bonuses on bills;
  - the union health plans and COBRA after one;
  - a week worked while claiming;
  - the talent routes to residence;
  - Ireland's Living Alone Increase;
  - a return home from abroad;
  - coursework's phone and clothes, taken as had;
  - the homebody and early riser trait texts.
- `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` marks 139 rows **Retired by the owner (2026-10-09)**. `docs/capabilities.json` retires
  17 of 18. `docs/WORLD.md`, `AUDIT.md`, `AI-INTEGRATION.md` and the catalog docs carry retirement banners, and a
  path a doc names without backticks is one that went.
- `tools/catalog-coverage.mjs` left the gate. `tools/social-lives.mjs` and the replay scripts in `probe/` (not
  tracked) still name deleted systems and haven't been adapted. The long replay and the multi-seed lives the owner
  asked for before the reset were interrupted by it and have not been run on this line.
- Content files used only by removed systems are still in `src/engine/content/`. They're data, read by nothing
  that runs. Pruning them is open.
- The Home page is the 2026-09-29 one. The rail around School, Banking, Daily Living and Character that the
  earlier brief described isn't built, because the owner's final word chose the 2026-09-29 setup.

## Start here: the transfer to Claude Account 2 (2026-09-23), and its first session (2026-09-24)

> **Newest of all: "give it downstream effects" (`31d0d98` to `78ee590`).** Midway through, the owner overruled the house rule's use as a reason to leave things out: "If it doesn't have any downstream effects, give it some and that goes for everything that doesn't because engines can be built for these things while also being implemented to run cohesively and logically." So "not kept, by design, because it changes nothing" is no longer an answer. Build the engine that makes it bite, from research. Earlier "not kept" items with only that reason are open again. Research committed: `research-customization.json`, `research-custom-effects.json`, `research-home-contents.json`, `research-home-effects.json`, `research-land-2.json`, `research-travel-2.json`, `research-travel-3.json`, `research-victoria.json`.

- BC Ferries between Vancouver and Victoria, with Victoria's hotels (`78ee590`).
- Vehicle theft at the published rates, recovered at the published share; full cover pays and liability doesn't (`78ee590`).

## The Final Master Build Command (from 2026-10-01): start here

The owner's command and its addendum ("unlimited" means the breadth of options within every feature) are the work now. Read `docs/BACKLOG.md` first: fifteen stages in dependency order, with acceptance criteria. Read `docs/AI-INTEGRATION.md` for the built-in assistant: it needs the owner's own Console API key, and their subscriptions can't power it.

Done and gated so far:
- **Stage 1, possessions** (`0c9a3415`): `systems/items.ts`, `systems/orders.ts` and the Belongings screen. Furniture migrates into items at load, and belongings follow every move.
- **Stage 2, catalogs** (`bd44d3cb`): `systems/catalog.ts`'s `arrange`, one builder per list in `systems/catalogs.ts`, and `ui/components/Catalog.tsx`. Every buy or book list is cheapest first, on a labeled basis.
- **Navigation** (`34d85a3f`): the rail's Pinned and Recent groups, and a page pin.

- **Stage 3, clothing, in part** (`e23a218c`): 1,198 real products from 74 retailers in America, Britain, Ireland, Canada and Mexico (`content/clothingcatalog.ts`, rebuilt by `tools/build-clothing.mjs` from the four `research-clothing-*.json` files). The garment is read from its name's head noun. Buying is in a store where SLATE has its address, or online at the retailer's fee, threshold and working days (`systems/clothing.ts`). New York's and Massachusetts's clothing exemptions are applied by item. Returns are unworn, inside the window. Dry cleaning is at real shops' lists (`content/garmentservices.ts`). Outfits are built from what's to hand, and worn-out garments are replaced on a free day. Garments are worn and washed one by one (`systems/wardrobe.ts`), replacing the 14-outfit counter, which a save from before wears into its starter wardrobe. Screen: Clothes, from Home's Home panel. Gate: 157 files, 2,180 tests; 43 mutations, all killed in the end. **Not yet:** alterations and fit (no body to fit, `docs/UNBITTEN.md`), size conversion, repair, resale, and packing into luggage.

- **Stage 4, food** (`b1bd404b` and the commit after it): real restaurants and their menus in the eight towns, at a table, taken away or delivered, with each town's meal tax, the tip and a delivery app's fees (`systems/dining.ts`, `content/restaurants.ts` built by `tools/build-restaurants.mjs`); a pint at a bar, Widmark's blood alcohol, a hangover, NHTSA's crash risk on every drive and each jurisdiction's first offense when stopped (`systems/alcohol.ts`, decider `alcohol.arrest`). Screen: Food. Then 4c: groceries as food items with FoodKeeper shelf life (512 products, 9 chains, `content/groceries.ts` built by `tools/build-groceries.mjs`), 44 recipes cooked from stock with leftovers, the day's meals taken from what's ready before groceries are bought, and a drink at home (`systems/kitchen.ts`, `systems/pantry.ts`). (`8d3ab15d`).
- **Stage 5, done** (`6e5bc091`, `ac00442c`): routines (`systems/routines.ts`); toiletries used up at weighed-use amounts, restocked on a free day, and 37 named barbers and salons (`systems/hygiene.ts`). The routines commit says 160 test files; the gate ran 161.
- **Stage 13, in part** (`63cf7d3d`, `f982b053`): a fitting before screen work, paid by each union's rule, and an actor's own outfits lent at the union's allowance (`systems/fittings.ts`, `FITTING` in `systems/career.ts`); the Primetime Emmys and BAFTA Film, at the odds their published fields give, lifting recognition (`systems/awards.ts`, decider `awards.vote`). The other bodies publish fields of films or productions, not performances, and aren't modeled; negotiation and press are researched, not built.
- **Luggage** (`faa55603`): every change of town goes through `moveTo` (`systems/luggage.ts`), which packs a carry-on with a week's clothes and the toiletries on leaving home and unpacks it on coming back. Until then a life on location had nothing to wear and no toothbrush every day away.
- **Stage 9, in part** (`a76e05ca`): care at 35 named providers, their own doctor where chosen, an uninsured urgent visit at a provider's self-pay price (`systems/clinics.ts`). Booking with waits, referrals and pharmacy prices are researched (`research-health-visits.json`), not built.
- **Stage 11, in part** (`8dc8eb7b`): utility setup charges where providers publish them, and power cuts at each distributor's published rate spoiling the fridge past four hours (`systems/utilities.ts`, decider `utilities.outage`).
- **Stage 6, in part** (`1bde1b5b`): 28 named hotels, 327 rooms, each at its own booking engine's rates for three nights read with Playwright (`systems/hotels.ts`, `tools/build-hotels.mjs --reduce` from the raw capture); Marriott, Hilton and Hyatt refused the read.
- **Stage 7, in part** (`6d03e264`, `becf9173`): stores sell in their own opening hours (`systems/errands.ts`); diesel at diesel's price, which had been charged at petrol's in America, Canada and Mexico City.
- **The guide caught up** (`1511ed1a`): the Home and Career chapters said nothing about the clothes, food, routines, fittings and awards they opened; each now has a paragraph. The Travel chapter now names hotels by name.
- **Stage 8, in part** (`f0b67fe1`): a driven car needs unscheduled repairs at RepairPal's 2026 rate for its model or make, severe at its share; an ordinary one at CarMD's $554.01, a severe one at three times the annual cost. A severe fault stops the car, a lit light fails New York's, Connecticut's and California's computer-read inspection, and either lowers the car's value. AAA's upkeep now has the expected repairs taken out of it. 28 named garages, a dealer for its own make only, booked in their own hours (`systems/repairs.ts`, `content/carshops.ts`, decider `vehicle.fault`). Not built: scheduled servicing, loaners, wraps, upholstery, audio, wheel fitment, the tint and lowering laws (`research-vehicle-shops.json`, which also corrects the NCT to €60 and California's lowering law as repealed).
- **Stage 10, two parts** (`3be73e11`, `5a686a49`): the people a life knows, the Yale class at the degree and a castmate from each job, named as their country names people (`content/people.ts`, decider `social.person`). Invitations to a meal or home are answered with a stated reason, and Hall's hours make friends. Living alone is in the life table at Holt-Lunstad's 1.32, spread so the table stays everybody's. A partner: asked out, a couple from the ninth date, moving in with the rent and bills split evenly, breaking up. Both of the backlog's acceptance tests pass. Screen: People. Not built: dating apps, marriage, divorce, children (`research-relationships.json`).

USPS's hold keeps packages (read on its FAQ), and every post's hold keeps a licence or ID card in the post. Sitters at Rover's nightly price in five towns. Burglary is unchanged: no rate for an empty-looking home is published.
      - Washing up (`ec2c6680`). The day's dishes go in the sink without a running dishwasher. Wash Up takes ENERGY STAR's 230 hours a year (38 minutes a day's) and the American Cleaning Institute's teaspoon of dish soap a sinkful, which made dish soap a used-up supply. A full sink, three days', refuses cooking.
      - A car sold to a dealer for less, or listed on the market's site (`ffc44fb1`, `systems/carsale.ts`). Britain's 10–15% gap; America's from KBB's one worked example as a fixed $3,815; AutoTrader.ca's 30 days.
      - Gas and water by tariff (`90c1e455`, `systems/gas.ts`, `systems/water.ts`). America's gas use was derived from EIA's Natural Gas Annual (`derived-gas-use.json`). Water is billed as each town bills it: a London occupier, elsewhere a house's owner.
      - Rooms (`c322ef0f`, `systems/rooms.ts`). England's space standard gives a let room 11.5 m² and built-in storage by bedrooms at 2.3 m. Half a floor can be furnished, by each IKEA piece's footprint, a furnished home's implied pieces included. A flat-share's bathroom is shared, with a new decider, `rooms.shower`.
      - A haircut lifts the mood by how due it was, the last visit's first reader (`ba069d66`).
      - Repeat prescriptions (`5c03c083`): England's ordered from the GP with two weeks left and collected 5 working days on; British Columbia's good two years, then renewed by a doctor's visit, booked and kept. Writing it found Ontario's 100-day supply before a trip had never applied: the atlas calls Ontario `ca-08`, not `ca-on`.
      - Furniture assembly (`e55fb289`): an IKEA piece flat-packed until put together, by IKEA's service at its published price or by you in an hour.

    - **Clubs and lessons as a lifecycle** (the commit after the audit, `systems/clubs.ts`, `ui/components/ClubsPanel.tsx`, `tests/clubs-going.test.ts`):
      - The town's clubs compared by a session's price, cheapest first.
      - Joined asking each time by default, with each meeting answered Going or Not Going.
      - The clock stops at the time to set out (`club.due`, `stopsClock`), and `club.go` is a trip there on foot, by transit, or by car with its tank and meter. Check-in is `club.check_in`, also a stop on Run Errands, and a meeting can be joined until halfway.
      - Staying after meets more of the club. A meeting not gone to is missed, settled at its end (`club.close`).
      - An outdoor club doesn't meet in a thunderstorm, read from the weather model.
      - A season is renewed by hand or by preference.
      - Going on its own is a preference (`club.set`) on a trip's rules.
      - Memberships from before keep going on their own, and their meetings gain the missing close item at load.
      - Transit is new to trips (`TripMode`, `transitFare`): the town's adult fare a leg, at rail's 30-minute estimate.
      - Gate 2,992 tests, exit 0; 11 mutations, all killed. Checked in the built app: the panel, its alignment, and Set Out Now.
      - Still open in the row: a lesson's discipline as an ability something reads, and providers' refund terms.

      - A fill is chosen as full, a quantity, up to an amount of money, or to a level (`planFill`), and each fill is kept on the car.
      - Who fills it as the month goes is each car's own preference (`vehicle.set_filling`). A car bought now is filled only by you, and burns its everyday kilometers a day at a time (`vehicle.day`, a system item), with that day's errands taken off them. A month of New York's driving is more than a tank.
      - Each station has its grades, and its own posted price where one is published (six of 23), standing from the day it was read. 40 named chargers carry their connectors, power and price, and charging slows past 80% by measured curves (`content/fuelcharging.ts`, built by `tools/build-fuel-charging.mjs`).
    - **Shopping** (`203f6d5f`): it stays in the car or in your arms until it's put away (`items.put_away`), and food in the car isn't cooked from. A fridge and freezer hold their country's liters (`content/coldstorage.ts`).
    - **Bags** (`3c016d57`): any number of bags go, an empty one too, each unpacked by itself. A room keeps what's unpacked there. Leaving without repacking leaves it held 90 days (`items.recover`). Checked bags are weighed at the counter by the departure country's flag carrier (`content/baggage.ts`). `moveTo` takes the simulation now.
    - **Every shortcut a person's own** (`8b665d42`, `systems/automation.ts`, Your Shortcuts on Routines): putting the shopping away, packing, and a free day's laundry, washing up, tidying, restocking and replacing worn clothes. Outdoors: an outdoor kitchen cooked in, a raised bed and a greenhouse planted and picked into the kitchen by real frost dates, and a pitch drilled on alone and lit.

Standing memories that bind this work: catalogs cheapest first; acting is the only career, with hired services allowed; "unlimited" means breadth of options.

>
> **Earlier: "Add it all" (`97ee85e` to `f5639dd`).** The owner asked what from the world-simulation spec hadn't been added, then said: "Add it all and don’t stop until its done." This box covers the nine commits since `3773ab3`. Each module was researched, tested, mutation-tested and gated before the next began. Research is in `docs/research/world/`: `research-stays-rentals.json`, `research-documents-safetynet.json`, `research-welfare.json`, `research-car-buying.json`, `research-vehicle-kinds.json`, `research-vehicle-insurance.json` and `research-kind-effects.json`.

**Built:**
- **Boats and planes, as many as the bank allows** (`97ee85e`): `boatsOf` and `planesOf` lists, each with its own loan and sale. Old saves move their one boat or plane into the list.
- **Kinds of stay** (`acdbe9b`): motels, budget hotels, extended-stay suites and hostels where read; flexible and prepaid rate plans; a missed night's refund; hotel parking; a round trip by one's own car.
- **Rentals** (`f1c2658`): a rental's class, by Kayak's prices at each airport, and the borders it may cross (US to Canada yes, US to Mexico no).
- **Assistance** (`38673bd`, `bf2b37f`): Ontario Works, BC Income Assistance and SNAP onto an EBT card, at their published rates, limits and earnings rules. This is the Canadian safety net the Toronto life below lacked.
- **At the dealer** (`88913a7`):
  - A new American car sells 3.4% below its sticker (KBB, August 2026).
  - The dealer's documentation fee: New York's and California's caps, Connecticut's average.
  - An American lease at Toyota Financial Services' published terms: returned, or bought out at its residual.
- **Motorcycles and scooters** (`54d8893`):
  - 36 models, taxed and insured by engine size.
  - The motorcycle license, a second track through the car's licensing code, on the same card.
  - A rider injured at NHTSA's 391 per 100 million miles against a car occupant's 86.
- **Motorhomes, campervans and vans** (`758de97`): a campground on a trip in one's own motorhome, at the region's named park; a move in one's own van instead of the movers.
- **Bicycles, e-bikes and ATVs** (`f5639dd`): Go For A Ride, and Ride The ATV on acreage one owns.

**Gate at `f5639dd`:** 1,983 tests. Every mutation was killed, or its survivor made into a test or a removed guard.

**Still to build from the owner's spec,** each needing its research read first. Most of it is already in the scratchpad or `docs/research/world/`:
- **Customization:** tint's legal limits, wraps, wheels, audio, accessibility, performance, and what insurers must be told.
- **A home's contents:** IKEA by market, lighting, flooring, kitchens, baths, entertainment, security with its insurance discount, pools, an EV charger, and each item's life and effect.
- **Construction choices:**
  - Professionals' fees, cost by spec, NAHB's line items and Cost vs Value.
  - Site work, demolition, multi-unit and condominium, retention and payment schedules, occupancy.
- **Land:**
  - Commercial land, agricultural values, a waterfront premium, islands.
  - Utilities and connection, easements, land loans, deposits, leasing land out.
  - Foreign ownership: Canada's ban to 2027-01-01, Mexico's fideicomiso.
- **Travel:**
  - Ferries, local transit's fares and caps, walking, taxis against rideshare.
  - The airport's steps, missed connections, compensation (UK261, EU261, APPR) and bag fees.
- **Documents:** national and state ID cards, the REAL ID rule, residence documents, title records.
- **Canada as an acting market (done 2026-09-30):** Toronto and Vancouver are career cities, with ten Canadian agencies at 15%, the IPA's and the BCMPA's own rates, the Canadian Theatre Agreement's, ACTRA's apprentice and membership and its dues, and Casting Workbook. Two fixes came with it: a Londoner's own British work was withheld as a visitor's, and any non-SAG-AFTRA job, London's and Dublin's included, joined American Actors' Equity. Still not built: ACTRA's use fees and residuals, commercials and voice, UBCP's own fees, and a non-member's permit fee.

**Land-values fix.** The played London life found ten acres of English farmland at £9,300 in 2077, because one year's published change had been compounded forever. Now a lot moves with the town's home market (`marketAt`), and farmland takes its latest change for a year, then the market (`landValue`).

**Stage 5a** (`systems/construction.ts`, `content/construction.ts`, research in `docs/research/world/research-construction.json`): a house on a lot one owns in New York, New Haven, Los Angeles or London.
- Sizes and costs: the Census's 2025 sizes and contract price a square foot for the region; London's space standard and SLATE's labeled cost.
- Bulk limits: R2's 0.75, the Baseline Mansionization Ordinance's 45%, RS-2's 30% coverage.
- Fees: each place's own.
- Stages: NAHB's seven, billed as each is done; an unpaid bill stops the work, and `build.pay` resumes it.
- Finished: the house becomes a home owned (`ownBuilt` in `homes.ts`).
- London's self-build levy is clawed back on a sale or letting within three years.

It shows on the Land screen's Build A House panel, with the guide chapter Building A House. `tools/screenshot.mjs` gained `SHOT_STEPS` (press, row, fill, advance, shot) to reach screens that need time. Gate: 106 files, 1,780 tests; 21 mutations, all killed.

Background batches get stopped by Claude Code's memory reaper: this machine has about 3 GB free, with OneDrive using 1.5 GB. `probe/summarize-lives.mjs`, `probe/ui-lives.mjs` (the built game walked on saves) and `probe/ui-create.mjs` round it out.

> **Newest of all, 2026-09-28, the three remodels.** The owner said "Do those three large remodels." Two commits, each gated:
>
> - **Body weight** (`31812d9`, `content/body.ts`, `systems/body.ts`). Every person has a height and a weight, drawn from the birth country's adults (NCD-RisC) or given at creation (imperial for a US birthplace, with a read-only body mass index). Weight follows a habitual intake, plus 134 kcal a meal eaten out, against Mifflin–St Jeor at the midpoint of the sexes' constants and the Compendium's MET values, moving at Hall's pace (23.9 kcal a day per kg, half-life 365 days). A working day is eaten back; a workout isn't; a broke day eats 40/76. BMI raises mortality (GBMC bands) and the onset of diabetes, hypertension and arthritis (Guh's ratios on the CDC's and Olmsted's rates). The `appetite` personality knob. Deciders `body.draw` and `health.onset`.
> - **Injury** (`31812d9`, `content/injury.ts`, `systems/injury.ts`). Rates per 1,000 hours for a run, the gym, a swim, the stage and the set, plus everyday rates from the NHIS. Mended in clinical times; laid up excuses work as a hospital stay does; injured at work, workers' comp at New York's and California's 2026 rates. Decider `health.injury`.
> - **Several credit cards at once** (the commit after it). `person.credit` is the record: the score, the late payments and a card on its way. It also carries the everyday card's own state (`primaryId`). Every other card's state is in `credit.cards[accountId]` (`CardState`), and `stateOf(person, card)` returns either one. Old saves need no migration: `ensurePrimary` sets the everyday card, and statement and due items without an `accountId` belong to it. A card can be applied for at any bank in the country that issues one, one per bank (`card.apply { bankId, transfer, fromId }`). Every card command takes an optional `accountId`, and `card.set_everyday` is new. When the everyday card closes, the next card takes its place. The record reads utilization across every card. A month paid as agreed counts once in total, each card adding its share, so the score now carries fractions and Home shows it rounded. A late payment on any card costs the full 50 points. On screen, the Cards tab shows one panel per card, an application panel with any issuer and a balance to move, each card's own offers, and each card under Your Cards. Home shows the combined total when several cards are held.
>

Screenshots were taken of one card, the application and two cards. Left in UNBITTEN, all SLATE's estimates: how the score shares a month across cards, one card per bank, and the unmodeled history-length effect. One thing seen in passing and not introduced here: in the comfortable New York probe, a Chase statement went unpaid one month (`missed=1`) with $42k in the bank, on one card and two alike. It is worth a look at `autopayReach`.

> **Newest of all, 2026-09-28, final: the housing items left open, finished.** The owner asked that every open item be finished and the weakest test made strong. Everything the homes and living entries of UNBITTEN listed as "not modeled" is built, with figures from three research agents:
>
> - **The return on a sale.** Depreciation taken is recaptured at up to 25% (§1(h)(6); `homeGain1250`, taxed at the lesser of 25% and the federal tax it adds, in NIIT and in the state's income); a let home's held passive losses are all freed the year it's sold or foreclosed (§469(g); `rentalDisposed`); a main home's exclusion is cut by the share of ownership before it was first lived in (§121(b)(5)).
> - **Rates move and mortgages refinance.** Starting at Freddie Mac's 7.03% (US) and Moneyfacts' 5.64% five-year fix (UK) for September 2026, rates walk month by month (decider `homes.rates`, `rateShift`: at most 0.2 points a month, pulled a thirtieth back). `home.refinance` pays the loan off with a new one at today's rate over a full term, for 3% closing costs.
> - **Scotland and Wales.** Their own council tax (Scotland's 1991 bands and shares, Band D £1,653.44; Wales's 2003 bands with a ninth, I, Band D £2,283), their own transfer taxes (LBTT with the 8% ADS; LTT with its higher rates). A flat's council tax is split by its bedrooms, the flatmates.
> - **Inside the home.** Fixtures move an owned home's letting rent and its listing price (ApartmentAdvisor's premiums: in-unit laundry 5.57%, gym 4.86%, balcony 3.98%, AC 1.30%, dishwasher 1.07%). A rented flat keeps its published rent. Bills leave out the summer's cooling a home can't run (`COOLING_USE`) and add each washer and dryer load's electricity (DOE/EIA at 18.19¢). Renters insurance (`home.renters_insurance`, state premiums from the Triple-I) covers burglary (FBI, 8.2 a thousand homes a year) and a renter's building fire over a $500 deductible.
> - **The strong buzz test.** `showcaseBuzz` is exported from the career week and tested at its function: 400 months, the share within 0.6–1.4× of `BUZZ.chance`, and nothing without a showcase, past 180 days, below every bar or already managed.
>
> Tests in `tests/homes-open.test.ts`, `tests/homes.test.ts` and `tests/records-bite.test.ts`. UNBITTEN's stale "property tax charged to no one" is corrected: an owned home pays it at the engine's rate. The non-housing entries of UNBITTEN (the crafts, the performer title and others) are still open. Verified: the gate passed with 1,493 tests in 71 files; seventeen mutations of the new claims were all killed (six survived the first run and got the tests that kill them: the 25% rate, the listing premium, the rate walk's pull and its use, the machines on the bills, and a renter's fire).

> **Newest of all, 2026-09-28, after living in a home: the whole-game sweep.** Four read-only audit agents (screens against the engine; cross-system cohesion; money and law; unread state) reported ~75 findings. Fixed in `a9b7e30` (the homes screens made true of the engine) and the commit after it: relocation, eviction, foreclosure and sale go to an empty home of theirs before a room or a sofa; a pending purchase or a job away blocks relocation; rent paid ahead is refunded on leaving; a former landlord's debt is no new landlord's notice; the place stands still while they're away; outdoor actions read the hour's weather as the panel does; rent received is income for the benefit tests; what's owed on homes counts in net worth; a mortgage shortfall is no longer taken for a bank's charge-off; a loan payment reaches savings only where linked, with its fee. Law: England's 5% surcharge on an additional home; Canada's tiered down payments and 20% to let; Ireland's and New Zealand's 30% to let; Ireland's 3.5 times for a buyer before; Northern Ireland's one-month deposit; the lender's 45% and 620 honestly sourced; a filing penalty to 22.5% on its own; 2026's long-term gain brackets; mortgage insurance's phase-out; depreciation not sheltered by the exclusion; a foreclosure's gain on the return; the $750,000 interest cap across homes; escrow ending at payoff without double tax; a tenant's deposit owed back when the bank can't; Band D for 2026–27, £2,392. Records that the screens show are listed as such in UNBITTEN; dead fields removed.

> **Newest of all, 2026-09-28, last: living in a home.** The owner asked for homes to be lived in: named kinds and the real fixtures of each, used every day, following where the home is, backed by research. Research ran in three parallel agents (fixtures and chores; renting and moving law; sizes and amenities), and every figure is sourced in the code's comments or marked SLATE's estimate.
>
> - **Kinds**: studio, apartment (a condo when owned in North America and Australia, a flat in Britain), penthouse (the top of a dense city's market, with a terrace), townhouse, house, mansion (5,000 sq ft and up; a country house in Britain). `isFlat` replaced the studio-or-condo checks.
> - **Fixtures** (`Fixtures`, `drawFixtures` in `content/homes.ts`): bath, dishwasher, laundry (in-unit, washer only, the building's, none), cooling (central, units, none; America's by census region), outdoor (balcony, terrace, yard), gym (building, home). Listings carry them; a rented place is drawn at the move (`home.unit`, decider `homes.rental`).
> - **Daily life** (`systems/dwelling.ts`, `life.look_after`): Cook A Meal / Eat A Meal, Order In (seven times the cost, LendingTree), Shower, Take A Bath (a tub), Work Out (a gym builds 1.2×, a fine-day run 1.1×, home 1×), Swim In The Pool (a warm day), Sit Outside (fine weather, a balcony, terrace or yard), Unwind, Do The Laundry (the home's way: own machines, a rack overnight, the building's for $3.50 a load, the laundromat for two hours and $5.75), Clean The Place (by size; ATUS). A wardrobe of fourteen outfits, one a day and one a workout; out of clean clothes the morning starts less fresh and the day weighs. Tidiness falls six a day (less with a dishwasher); under 40 it weighs. A free day keeps up roughly on its own; a working day (four hours of commitments) doesn't. `home.cleaner` hires a weekly cleaner ($45/hr, two hours at least).
> - **Sleep**: a hot night without air conditioning sleeps worse (Minor et al. 2022: a thirtieth of a night over 25°C, a twentieth over 30°C).
> - **Upkeep retuned to the Triple-I's claim rates**, and fire added.
> - **Deposits** held to each place's law (`depositCapMonths`): New York and California one month even for a poor record, Connecticut two, England five weeks, Germany and Iceland three, and more. Until now only Britain's cap was read.
> - The owner also set a standing standard for the screens: evenly aligned and compactly spaced (`.stats` grid, a test in `tests/interface.test.ts`).
>
> **Next, as the owner asked:** a sweep of the whole game so nothing is decoration and everything flows as one world.
>
> **Newest of all, 2026-09-28, later: "the full housing experience".** The owner asked for housing as full as everything else. Built on the homes of `d6dd351`, in one gated commit:
>
> - **The market turns over.** Each of a town's fifty places sells on its own one-to-four-month cycle and a new home comes on (decider `homes.listings` by slot and generation); a home unsold two months is cut 3%, at four 3% again.
> - **Offers.** A price, a down payment, to live in or to let, with or without an inspection. The seller accepts, counters at halfway or refuses by how near the asking price it is, how long the home has sat and how hot the market is (`sellerFloor`, decider `homes.market`). 1% earnest money goes into escrow; the inspection's report (every part's age) comes on day five and the earnest comes back if called off by day ten; closing on day thirty, with a financing contingency.
> - **Several homes.** `person.homes` replaced `owned` (`migrate` moves an old save's). A home not lived in is an investment: 15% down, 0.625% on the rate; a lender counts 75% of rent. Moving out or relocating leaves a home owned and empty; `home.move_in` moves back.
> - **Letting** (`home.let`, `home.end_tenancy`): market rent by bedrooms (near HUD's ratios), a tenant found sooner nearer the market, a month's deposit held as a liability, rent on the first, renewals two in three at market or a move-out with damage kept from the deposit. A backyard cottage lets on its own.
> - **Upkeep** (decider `homes.upkeep`): worn parts fail, likelier with the cube of wear; storms and gales damage worn roofs and hard freezes burst an empty home's pipes, read from the weather; insurance pays over a deductible, never wear, and a claim loads the premium for three years. Big work weighs on whoever lives there.
> - **Borrowing** (`home.borrow`): a home equity loan to 80% of value less the mortgage, fifteen years.
> - **Credit**: a mortgage paid on time adds to the record; thirty days late is reported.
> - **Council tax** in England, by 1991 band from Band D's 2025–26 average, 25% off alone, ten instalments; renters pay it too.
> - **The return** (`systems/taxreturn.ts`): itemizing when a home's interest (to $750,000 borrowed), property tax and state taxes (the 2025 act's cap and phase-down) and from 2026 mortgage insurance beat the standard deduction; rent less costs and 27.5-year depreciation, a loss only within the passive allowance, carried forward; a sale's gain with the $250,000 exclusion after two of five years, long-term at 0/15/20% plus the 3.8% investment tax.
> - **Bills** of a home owned grow with its size; solar halves them and a pool adds its upkeep.
>
> The owner then asked for homes to be lived in: named kinds (apartment, townhouse, house, penthouse, mansion) with the fixtures of each (a shower, a kitchen to cook in, a bed, a washer and dryer where there's one), used every day, and the fixtures following where the home is. That is the next module, and after it a sweep of the whole game so nothing is decoration.
>
> **Newest of all, 2026-09-28: "Build it all".** The owner asked for the three things part B had left out. Each is its own gated commit.
>
> - **The pension, finished** (`1e38466`). A production counts to the Plan's $232,000 ceiling (`sagByProject`), a pension can start early from 55 with ten credits (`pension.start`, a quarter of a percent less a month under 65), and it goes on the return (`recordPension`).
> - **A commercial agent** (`e509411`, `systems/commercial.ts`). CESD, DDO and Abrams sign on presence and record, send the commercial auditions (the theatrical agency then sends none), and are left on the commercials contract's own rule: after 120 days with no offer, then under $4,000 in 91. Renewed for three years when it booked one.
> - **Homes** (`systems/homes.ts`, `content/homes.ts`, `content/homevalues-us.ts`, `ui/workspaces/HomesWorkspace.tsx`, route `/workspace/homes`). The owner asked mid-task for "50 homes per city in every state/region/country from cheapest to most expensive … real homes and addresses plus infinite amount of renovations catered to what that specific home could get". What was built:
>   - **Fifty a town, cheapest first**, drawn per save (decider `homes.listings`) about the town's typical value: Zillow's Home Value Index for 12,485 US towns (`tools/build-home-values.mjs`, August 2026), the state's measured price-to-rent for other US towns, and SLATE's estimate by country elsewhere. Types are named as the country names them (a flat in Britain, a condo in America), sizes in square feet or meters.
>   - **Addresses are not real listings.** Real people's homes and scraped listings were declined (their privacy, and Zillow's terms): each home is a common street name of its country with a number of SLATE's, labeled as the game's.
>   - **Buying**: an offer, and a closing thirty days on. A lender reads the record (620) and two years' income (43% debt-to-income in America; 4.5 and 4 times income in Britain and Ireland). England's Stamp Duty Land Tax with first-time relief is real; other closing costs, rates and down payments are SLATE's estimates by country.
>   - **Owning**: the mortgage on the first with the tax and insurance in escrow, mortgage insurance dropped at 78% (Homeowners Protection Act); cash owners pay tax in April and October halves. Property tax reads the place's rate and California's 2% cap. Late: a fee at 15 days, the record at 30, foreclosure at 120, the sale at 210, and the sofa. Net worth counts the home.
>   - **Renovations without end**: 38 jobs, each tied to what the home has (a deck built can be rebuilt, a garage converted has no door). What a job adds is its return at sale times the wear of the part it replaces, so every part wears and can be done again. Costs and returns are SLATE's estimates: the Remodeling cost-against-value report's terms forbid its figures in software, so only its pattern was used.
>   - **Selling**: listed, closed 45 days on, the agents and the loan paid, and a move into the rented home chosen.
>   - **Not built** (in `docs/UNBITTEN.md`): the mortgage interest and property tax deductions, a sale's gain on the return, the record rising with mortgage payments, letting a home (so a backyard cottage earns nothing, and moving city waits for a sale), and Britain's banded council tax.
>
> Verified: the gate passed with 1,409 tests; eighteen mutations of the homes claims were all killed; the screen was checked at phone and desk width (`probe/homes-shot.mjs`, untracked). The owner still merges the branch to `main` themselves.
>
> **Newest, 2026-09-28, last: "everything that would make it perfect".** Account 2 did this at the owner's word. Each part below was its own gated commit.
>
> **A. The school**
>
> - **Stage work in the evaluation** (`350c362`). The evaluation reads the work on stage: a run mostly off falls short (`workFloor`), and a strong one is noted. The School's night bars are a student's, and the Rep's a professional's.
> - **The director's note** (`350c362`) names what to work on.
> - **The Rep's unannounced seasons** (`350c362`) are dealt from `REP_PLAYS`.
> - **The Cabaret and the Summer Cabaret** (`2cc9c57`) have parts, plays (`SUMMER_PLAYS`), nights and a note.
> - **Teachers' notes** (`9896f39`). Each course's teacher gives a note at the evaluation (`teachersNotes`).
> - **Bonds with classmates** (`b4de8aa`). Classmates' `bond` makes them friends or rivals. Friends in a cast lift a night, and a rival taking the lead is named.
>
> **B. The business**
>
> - **Recognition and festivals** (`a9f85b8`). Recognition comes when the work is seen, by its audience. Television counts when it airs. An independent film gets into Tribeca one time in five, the Brooklyn Film Festival otherwise, or goes to streaming (`FILM_ROAD`, decider `career.selection`).
> - **The class after Yale** (`29efaa4`, `systems/rivals.ts`, decider `career.rivals`). The class is signed at the showcase and books work monthly. A classmate is in the room at auditions now and then, and a better read takes the part. Deadline and Playbill carry it on Trade News and Your Class.
> - **A commercial's product** (`d8c22fb`, `PRODUCTS`). It is read by Animals, Cars, Fashion, Nature and Politics (`PRODUCT_INTEREST`).
> - **The SAG-AFTRA pension** (`d76ed1d`, `systems/pension.ts`). Credits, vesting and a pension from 65. An old save's day-job fund is paid out then too.
> - **Not built, with the reasons:**
>   - The commercials contract's own 91-day clause is not modeled. SLATE signs both contracts together, and the union then applies the TV/theatrical rules.
>   - Property tax needs a home to own, a whole system, and is the owner's to ask for.
>
> **C. Polish**
>
> - **Sources folded.** The sources and estimates sit behind a "Sources And Estimates" line (`Fine`).
> - **Phone width.** The screens fit a phone's width: the tabs wrap, table words stay whole, and wide tables scroll in their panel. `probe/mobile.mjs` (untracked) checks every screen.

> **Newest, 2026-09-28, later: the playthrough and its fixes.**
>
> **The playthrough.** `probe/play-life2.ts` (untracked) played twelve looked-after lives in the engine to July 2031, and two runs played the real screens, until low memory stopped them after their first legs. There were no errors, the ledger balanced, nobody was stranded, and every save reopened on the same minute.
>
> **What it found, and what was fixed:**
>
> 1. **Too easy.** All twelve lives finished first, with acting 95–97 and presence and composure at 99–100. The fixes:
>    - `classmateFocus` went from 0.85 to 1.
>    - `driveSpread` went from 0.15 to 0.25.
>    - `sessionGain` went from 0.55 to 0.42.
>    - A night now reads against what the part asks, at School or at the Rep (`nights.par`).
>    - Composure gains are smaller, and a remarkable night adds less presence.
>
>    Six lives since then graduate at about 89, against a best classmate of 81–85. One finished second. Nights are mixed, presence lands at 71–79 and composure at about 69.
> 2. **Sleep.** The Sleep dialog's suggestion was never under 3 hours, and slept through calls. It now suggests what is left before the call, half an hour at the least.
> 3. **Clashing castings.** A third-year in the Rep's company was also cast in a thesis. They now play only at the Rep that term, and so do classmates at the Rep.
> 4. **Repeated plays.** A play could come round twice. Each save now shuffles the list once and deals it out from the class's first year.
> 5. **Offers that can't be taken.** `stillTakeable` drops the Take It from an offer once a clashing job is taken.
> 6. **Notices.** Statement notices are off by default, but a statement with a payment due always notifies.
> 7. **Stale text.** The welcome, the guide and character creation said the player was "the one the faculty wanted most". Corrected.
> 8. **Names.** Given names now appear once in a class, as family names already did.
> 9. **Unclaimed money.** The bank now writes 90 days before handing an account to the state (`ESCHEAT_NOTICE_DAYS`, the uniform act's 60 to 120 days). Keep The Account there reactivates it.
>
> **Newest, 2026-09-28: the school made as perfect as the owner asked, in six parts.** Account 2 found six failings in the school on the owner's question, and the owner said "Do all 6". The commits are `aeb08ee` and the one after it.
>
> 1. **The class competes.** Admits differ in overall talent (`ESTIMATE.talent`). The player is drawn from the class's top quarter, not above everyone. Each classmate has their own drive and is an actor or an actress (`Classmate`). `mateOf` fills both in for older saves.
> 2. **Casting reads the skills.** Each play asks for its own skills (`content/plays.ts`, `readOf`). The chair places the class on the term's first play, then the best read takes the biggest part written for them (`partsFor`), with the balance in `castScore`.
> 3. **Real plays.** Each save deals its own season of public-domain plays with their own characters (`playOf`), because the seasons the player is cast in aren't announced. A festival's new plays stay unnamed, with parts by size.
> 4. **A night has a result.** `nightOf` (decider `school.night`) reads each night off, solid, strong or remarkable. The run keeps its nights, the director gives a note on the last one, and `lastWork` carries it to the next casting.
> 5. **Understudying.** On call is a call at five that needs nobody at the theater and stops no clock. Sent on, the understudy is told, and the curtain goes on their calendar. Every understudy gets a run-through on the run's second dark Monday.
> 6. **Classmates as people.**
>    - The whole class is cast each term, with the player's castmates named and "Around The Class" on the Casting tab.
>    - Classmates grow from their productions (`COMPANY_KIND`).
>    - The third year's Rep company takes classmates too.
>    - Each classmate has their own family name.
>
> Over six three-year runs, players began from first to eighth and played everything from The Moon to Lady Macbeth. One finished with no agency. The owner is walking through the school screen step by step, and is at Casting.
>
> **Parked by the owner, 2026-09-27: what is left, to come back to after the school walkthrough.** The owner asked for this list to be saved as it stood, and is first going through the school screen step by step, with the owner deciding each step. Come back to this list after that walkthrough. The recommendation given was the first item, real competition. It is a large remodel, so the owner sets the plan.
>
> 1. **Gameplay gaps a player would notice.**
>    - An audition is a roll against a part's bar, and no rival actors compete for it. Only the player and the classmates have skills.
>    - Classmates are never cast against the player, and have no careers after school.
>    - A part asks only what its genre asks: a drama that needs a fight or a song asks for neither.
>    - Recognition comes only from finished work, not its audience. Overscale is a single multiple of scale, not a negotiated quote.
>    - No festival ever passes over a film.
>    - There is no trade news.
>    - The commercials contract's own 91-day clause ($4,000) is not modeled.
> 2. **Things that are said back and nothing reads.**
>    - Most crafts, and the interests Animals, Cars, Fashion, Nature and Politics.
>    - The performer title.
>    - Property tax.
>    - An older save's pension fund.
> 3. **Figures waiting on publication.**
>    - The 2027–28 union rates, Off-Broadway categories B and C, the Commercials Contracts' third year, and animation's 2026 rate.
>    - SAG-AFTRA's 2027 COBRA rate, New York City's 2026 benchmark premium, and California's 2026 penalty.
>    - Yale Rep's later seasons and the school's later dates.
>    - Some banks' fees and fraud rates.
> 4. **Banking fine print.**
>    - French card withdrawal ceilings.
>    - Bank of America's check order.
>    - Card bonus categories beyond groceries and bills.
>    - One card at a time.
>    - Dormant accounts are never closed.
>    - A year's energy by the climate.
>    - Other countries' holidays.
> 5. **The owner's to decide.**
>    - Merging this branch into `main`.
>    - A Base44 deployment.
>    - Taking out `SlateOfficialResult`.

>
> **Newest, 2026-09-27, later: the attributes engine, the ways to build it, and the 21 skills.** All by Account 2 (session `session_01AmQ8DKKk8371N6NckRHRbY`).
>
> - **The attributes** (`1242e3c`, `50846d2`), `systems/attributes.ts`: presence, composure, reputation and recognition on `person.attrs`. It is not `attributes` or `reputation`, which the load's `SHED` list deletes. Presence is drawn once and read at every audition and the showcase. Composure sets the nerves an audition, performance or day on set costs. Reputation scales the auditions an agency sends and reads beside a showing. Recognition pays above scale and fades monthly. Panels are on School → Standing and Career → Representation.
> - **Building them** (`8ad9b59`, `1ef98b1`): an improv course, a casting director workshop, a publicist (locked until a screen credit or Broadway), festival premieres for a finished film, and a coach halving an audition's nerves.
> - **The offer and casting fixes** (`fe68714`): `acceptRefusal` is shared by accepting, the offer decision and the Offers list, and a finished run reads as finished.
> - **The 21 skills.** The craft was seven areas and is now 21 skills, listed in alphabetical order in `SKILLS` (`content/school.ts`): Accents And Dialects, Audition Technique, Character Work, Classical Text, Clown And Mask, Cold Reading, Comedy, Dance, Emotional Access, Improvisation, Intimacy, Memorization, Movement, On Camera, Scene Study, Script Analysis, Singing, Speech, Stage Combat, Voice and Voice-Over.
>   - **What reads them.** A role's `reads` (`READS`) and its genre (`GENRE_READS`) set `levelFor`. The audition's own skills (`auditionSkills`) are a fifth of the read. `actingOf` (scene study twice, character, emotional access) is what the showcase and the Yale Rep role read, with `ESTIMATE.showcaseSeen`.
>   - **What grows them.** Each course trains several skills. Work grows what it reads (`growsFor`). An audition grows its own skills, improv grows improvisation, and a workshop grows cold reading.
>   - **The genre.** It is now drawn when the audition is set, so the breakdown's genre is read at the audition. Before, it was drawn at the offer.
>   - **Old saves.** `upgradeCraft` carries a save's seven areas into the 21 on load, with no schema move.
>   - **Calibration.** It was measured against the build before, over five seeds. `castAtRep` went from 80 to 77, since the old one number ran about three points above the new acting by the third year. The same showcase run scores 76.7, against 76.9 before, and all eight agencies still offer.
>   - **Mutation testing.** All 24 mutations were killed. The gate passed with 1,340 tests.
> - **The agency contract's term** (`AGENCY_TERM`, `AGENCY_TERM_KIND`, `career.renew`), from SAG's Rule 16(g), under which SAG-AFTRA's franchised agents still sign actors. A first contract runs a year (§XI D(2)). In its last third (D(3)), an agency that booked the client work in the term offers three years (D(1a)), and one that booked nothing lets it run out; which it does is SLATE's estimate. Leaving follows Exhibit E ¶6: in a first contract's first 151 days only after 120 days in a row with no offer, and after that, or in a renewal, whenever the last 91 days held fewer than ten days' work. Before, the 91-day rule ran from the first day. A save signed before terms existed gets the one-year term running now, on its next career week. The playthrough's WME contract, signed April 23, 2029, would run to April 23, 2030.
> - **The playthrough** (`probe/playthrough.mjs` and `probe/playthrough-legs.mjs`, untracked). It played the UI from September 2027 to 20 September 2029 in six legs on the build before the skills. There were no page errors and no refused actions, and every save reopened on the same minute. The character graduated and scored 84.9 and 86.3 at the showcases. They signed with WME and went to 60 auditions, 8 of them booked. They worked a co-star day and a commercial, and accepted an off-Broadway musical and a LORT Broadway play. By then, presence was 80, composure 88.6, reputation 38 and recognition 0.9.
>
> **Newest, 2026-09-27: the cohesion audit.** The owner asked that everything built flow as one cohesive, logical system with nothing decorative, "operating just how a top tier video game would." Seven read-only audits, with reports in the session's scratchpad and not in the repository, covered dead state, UI decoration, cross-system flow, and the screens area by area. A played-life probe (`probe/play-life.ts`, untracked) also ran five characters from enrollment to 2033 with the clock stopping for calls. It found no exceptions, an always-balanced ledger, wellbeing in range, live career, health and tax chains, and nobody stranded. The fixes land one module at a time:
>
> - **A hospital stay is excused** (`inHospital` in `systems/health.ts`). On screen or in the booth, the production recasts, pays what was worked and the agency keeps the client. On stage, the understudy covers and the run goes on. An agency audition missed in hospital is no strike. At school, a class is excused, and a shift, a rehearsal, a performance or a Summer Cabaret night is covered, with no breach. Until then, each of these read a stay as walking away.
> - **Where they live, not where they stand.**
>   - The day turns over on the home's clock. `rehome` in `systems/household.ts` runs on every move of the player's. The pending rollover goes to now after a move east and to the new midnight after a move west, so no date turns over twice.
>   - A move on the 1st before the evening's rent pays the month once, and a room found from the sofa starts paying next month.
>   - `home.move` and the sofa's own move wait until they're home, and Home says so while they're away.
>   - `homeCityOf` is the home's city, so the place look's away lines show.
>   - These now read `homeOf` in place of where they stand:
>     - `bank.switch` and its rules;
>     - re-banking after an overdraft closure;
>     - an unemployment claim, which is certified from anywhere in the US as an interstate claim is, and not from abroad;
>     - the life table.
> - **The record without a card:** `reportWithoutCard` in `systems/credit.ts`, on the 1st. A score with no card open ages back `NO_CARD.aging` (2) a month to a thin file's `ceiling` (680), both SLATE's estimates. It holds while anything is in collections or a payment was late in the year, and loses 10 a month with rent owed. Before this, a charge-off or a closed card froze it for life.
> - **A season picked up with nothing in the bank:** `joinFor` runs before the clash loop. On a job they're bound to, a fee the bank can't cover goes to `unions.owed`, which the next check pays as dues. Before this, it threw after the other job had been released, and both were lost.
> - **Interest is income:** `recordInterest` in `systems/earnings.ts`, from `payInterest` and `bank.take_term_interest`. It goes on the year's return (`YearEarnings.interest`, no payroll tax), into the month's income that Medicaid and the premium credit read, and into the residence share that the city tax reads.
> - **A payment that bounced isn't a payment:**
>   - Union dues the bank can't cover go to `unions.owed`, which the next check pays, and are on the return only once paid.
>   - A SAG-AFTRA or Equity-League premium unpaid ends the plan, which opens the COBRA offer.
>   - A sublet goes through `spend`, from the bank, then the card, or else a friend's sofa, and is a cost of the work.
>   - `EXPENSE_LINE` in `systems/household.ts` names the ledger's expense line by category. Before this, dues, premiums, tax and work costs all went to Food.
>   - A bank switch moves standing orders and payments set ahead onto the new accounts.
>   - `troubles` lists what money weighs with, and adds medical debt, collections and tax owed to the rent, an overdraft and a full card. The day's worry (`inTrouble`) and Home's All Clear both read it.
> - **Turns are felt:** `TURNS` and `feel` in `systems/enjoyment.ts` (SLATE's estimates). Morale moves by `moraleSwing`, and stress, where it rises, by `stressGain`. They cover:
>   - career: a booking offered, an agency signed, being let go, being replaced, a hospital recast, a season picked up or canceled;
>   - school: each standing below good, and the degree.
> - **An out-of-town client is put on tape:** an agency's audition for a client who lives outside its city is a self-tape. One who lives there but is away still owes the room.
> - **A commitment takes its hours:** `occupiedAt` in `systems/life.ts`. Inside a call the player turned up for, `life.sleep` and every `life.look_after` but a meal are refused.
>   - A day's work costs `workEnergy` by its hours (8 for eight), and a performance `performanceEnergy` (10, as the School's). Both are SLATE's estimates.
>   - `tsc -p .` checks nothing, because the root config only holds references; the typecheck is `tsc -b` (`npm run typecheck`).
> - **Payroll:**
>   - `paycheck` withholds a city's resident tax only from a resident (its `resident` option).
>   - The Summer Cabaret stipend has tax withheld as a paycheck does, FICA among it.
>   - Pay, residuals, the stipend, work-study, unemployment and a refund go through `payeeAccount` in `systems/cash.ts`: the open current account, or a check cashed into the wallet. Before this, all of them were dropped when no current account was open.
> - **Connecticut's safety net:** every life starts in New Haven, and before this a graduate there could get no cover and no unemployment. Now `BENEFIT_STATE` maps New Haven to `'ct'`, and `content/safetynet.ts` has the figures (sources in `ct-safetynet.md` in the session scratchpad, not in the repository):
>   - `CONNECTICUT_UI`: 40 times the weekly benefit; the two highest quarters over 26; $44 to $721; no waiting week; a partial week at two-thirds of its pay.
>   - HUSKY D.
>   - Covered Connecticut (`coveredCtShare`, 175% of the year before's poverty line).
>   - The benchmark, and `marketplace-ct`, a stay being the deductible, then its copay.
>   - `CONNECTICUT_ENROLLMENT`.
>   - Insulin at $25.
>   - Six-month renewals from January 2027.
>   - `PROGRAM_NAMES` names each state's programs.
> - **The body:**
>   - `catching` in `systems/health.ts` raises the flu's chance with sleep debt (to 2.5 times) and stress (to 1.8 times). These are SLATE's tempered reading of Prather 2015 and Cohen 1991.
>   - The flu keeps somebody down `CARE.fluDays` (5, the CDC's), which excuses a student whatever their condition.
>   - `redrawIfChanged` in `systems/birthdays.ts` draws the rest of the year's odds of the end again when `frailty` moves a quarter-band (`HealthRecord.endBand`).
>   - A day on set or on stage soils by `setGrime` an hour.
>   - Food and a meal are bought at the prices of the town they're in (`foodCostsOf`).
> - **Credit reporting as the bureaus have it:**
>   - A US card's late payment goes on the record, costing 50 and cutting the line, only once it's thirty days past due, at the next due date still short. The fee comes on the day. Other countries' cards report at once.
>   - Rent owed to a landlord is no longer reported, in `reportMonth` or `reportWithoutCard`; an eviction or collections still is.
> - **Holidays the engine reads:** `content/usholidays.ts` computes the Federal Reserve's holidays and SAG-AFTRA's as rules, because the calendar's holiday files are fetched at run time. A US bank's next business day skips the Fed's, and a screen or voice job's days skip SAG-AFTRA's, Good Friday and the day after Thanksgiving among them. Weather stays the calendar's, in UNBITTEN, with the reason.
> - **The screens, against the engine (the UI audit, first half):**
>   - The School screen offers the Cabaret, the Summer Company and an elective shift only where the engine will take them: `cabaretRefusal`, `summerRefusal` and `shiftRefusal` in `systems/school.ts`, which the commands read too.
>   - The Yale Account reads the term they're in.
>   - Work-study books the Bulletin's 150 assigned hours a year and no more (`assignedBooked`), and the Cabaret's week clears work-study shifts.
>   - Home: Taxes And Cover shows from the first term, the School panel stops saying "Next: Classes" after the degree, and the tax deadline is New York's.
>   - Career: the reel counts the filmed monologues, the week's submissions read the agency's city, and the union and work-cost figures are converted.
>   - Papers: its dollar figures are converted, and Enroll and File A Claim show only where the engine allows them.
>   - The guide's sentences on rent and the card, cover, casting, the Cabaret, pay, dental, the life table and interest days now say what the engine does.
> - **The screens, against the engine (second half):**
>   - `centsFromTyped` in `core/money.ts` reads every typed amount, a decimal comma as well as a point; every field misread "12,50".
>   - A card's look reads its rate from `cardTerms`.
>   - The Bank screen says Germany's interest and fees come at the quarterly closing and shows the quarter's fees so far. It gives a savings account's fee before opening, and a card's replacement fee in the save's money.

Closing savings calls off its standing orders; `settleFees` charges a German quarter's fees on closing; a second-chance account pays its own fee alone; tiers, floors and caps convert. `PENDING.paymentHoldDays` is read: a payment from another bank holds the credit it frees (`credit.paymentHolds`). >   - Verified: the gate at 1,290 tests; every mutant of each new rule killed (equivalent ones rewritten or tested); seeds life-1 to life-5 played to 2033 with `probe/play-life.ts`.
